ESSAY      II

Of  the  Liberty  of  the  Press.

NOTHING  is  more  apt  to  furprize  a  foreigner,
than  the  extreme  liberty,  which  we  enjoy  in  this
country,  of  communicating  whatever  we  pleafe  to  the

republican  or  monarchical  ;  in  Holland  and  Venice,

geous  or  prejudicial  to  the, public?

is  neither  wholly  monarchical,  nor  wholly  republican.

politics,  that  the  two  extremes  in  government,  liberty
